Document 082: 2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-16

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月16日

1/5 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（１次速報値） 
～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅マイナス成長も、景気回復の基調は維持 
本日内閣府から公表された 2026年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（１次速報）は前期比年率
▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった1。個人消費が前期比▲0.3％
と減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も減少し、成長率を押し下げている。一方、設備投資は前
期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えした。概ね事前の市場予想（前期比年率＋0.2％、
筆者予想：同▲0.4％）の範囲内であり、サプライズはない。 
今回減少した個人消費については、過度に悲観する必要はないだろう。４-６月期にみられた自
動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の一時的な押し上げが一巡したことに加え、７-９月期には台風
や大雨、長雨などの天候不順が相次いだことも下押し要因となったとみられる。一方、名目賃金
の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、家計の所得環境は足元で改善している。食料品
や日用品などを中心とした物価の高止まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、今回の減
少をもって個人消費の基調が悪化したと判断する必要はないだろう。 
一方、設備投資が高い伸びとなったことは好材料だ。今回の増加には前期の特殊要因の反動も
含まれているが、それを除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持す
るなか、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。企業
部門の底堅さが改めて確認されたと評価できる。 
このように、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰは４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となったが、減少幅は僅か
だ。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことに加え、今回の個人消費の減少に
は、耐久財需要の剥落や８～９月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因が影響したとみられる
点も重要だ。今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろ
う。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、設備投資を中心に企業部門は堅調さを維持している。家計と
企業の回復に濃淡はみられるものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価で
きる。

1 ＧＤＰ統計では、季節調整のかけ直しや基礎統計の改定に伴い過去の値も毎回改定される。25年４-６月期が前期比
年率＋0.8％（改定前＋0.5％）、７-９月期が▲1.5％（同▲1.5％）、10-12月期が＋1.1％（同＋1.1％）、26年１-３月期
が＋1.9％（同＋1.9％）、４-６月期が＋1.7％（同＋1.4％）などとなった。符号の変化等はみられず、景気認識に変更
を迫るほどではなかった。

### Page 2

2/5

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
今回のＧＤＰを評価する際に注意が必要なのは、輸入と公的在庫の動きである。 
４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を通
じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油が
放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt（前
期比年率寄与度▲2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げは、
公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じた。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって原油輸入が
回復したため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因となった。一方、輸入の回復に伴って国
家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小した。この
結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.5％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大き
く押し上げている。 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げ（前期比年率寄与度▲2.2％Pt）と公的在庫変動による押し上げ
（同寄与度＋2.0％Pt）は、ともに原油の供給環境が改善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別
に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が回避されたことである。ＧＤ
Ｐ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用可能
な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むしろ前
向きに捉えるべきだろう。

10-12月期はプラス成長を予想 
 先行きについても、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想する。７-９月期は４四半期ぶり
のマイナス成長となったが、耐久財特需の剥落や天候不順による一時的な下押しが和らぐことか
ら、10-12月期には再びプラス成長に戻る可能性が高い。名目賃金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が続く
なか、所得環境の改善が個人消費を下支えするとみられる。 
ただし、今後は物価上昇率の再加速が個人消費の重荷になると予想される。既往の原油高や原
材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波及しつつある。今後は食料品や日用品など
への価格転嫁がさらに進むことに加え、電気・ガス代も上昇するだろう。物価上昇率の加速によ
り、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服する恐れがある。個人消費は先行き持ち直すとみる
が、回復ペースは力強さを欠くとみられる。 
一方、企業部門については今後も底堅い推移が期待できる。企業収益が高水準を維持するな
か、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。輸入資本
財価格や建設コストの上昇は下押し要因となるものの、設備投資は緩やかな増加基調を続けると
予想する。また、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さやグローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大も、輸

### Page 3

3/5 
出や生産を下支えするだろう。 
このように、10-12月期は個人消費が緩やかに持ち直すことに加え、設備投資や輸出の増加が景
気を下支えし、再びプラス成長になると予想する。物価高による個人消費への下押しには注意が
必要だが、企業部門の堅調さが維持されることで、日本経済は緩やかな回復を続けるだろう。

需要項目別の詳細 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と２四半期連続で減少した。形態別では、耐久財消費が前期比▲
3.5％（４-６月期：同＋3.6％）、非耐久財消費が同▲0.2％（４-６月期：同▲1.9％）、半耐久財
消費が同▲1.4％（４-６月期：同＋0.4％）、サービス消費が同＋0.1％（４-６月期：同▲0.1％）
となっている。４-６月期には環境性能割の廃止を受けた自動車販売の増加や、27年４月の省エネ
基準強化を控えたエアコンの前倒し購入など、耐久財消費の特需がみられたが、７-９月期にはこ
うした押し上げ効果が一巡した。また、８～９月には記録的な豪雨や台風、長雨などの天候不順
が相次いだことが、外出手控えによる消費機会の減少や旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて下押し
に繋がったとみられる。また、非耐久財消費にも弱さがみられる。食料品や日用品の価格上昇が
続くなか、家計の節約志向が根強いことに加え、天候不順による季節商品の販売減少なども下押
しに効いた可能性がある。 
もっとも、名目賃金の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、所得環境は足元で改善し
ており、家計を取り巻く環境が悪化しているわけではない。食料品を中心とした価格水準の高止
まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、４-６月期には高校授業料の実質無償化拡充や学
校給食費無償化に伴う個人消費から政府消費への振り替わりなどの特殊要因があり、今回７-９月
期についても耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが大きく影響したとみられ
る。個人消費が力強さを欠いていることは確かだが、今回の減少をもって消費の基調が悪化した
と判断する必要はないだろう。 
先行きについては、耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが和らぐことに加
え、賃金上昇や雇用の改善が下支えとなることから、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直すと予想する。
ただし、26年度後半には既往の輸入コスト上昇の価格転嫁が進み、食料品や日用品などの値上が
りや電気・ガス代の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する可能性が高い。物価上昇率の再加速によ
って実質賃金の改善が一服するリスクがあり、消費の回復ペースは当面緩やかなものにとどまる
だろう。 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、３四半期ぶりに増加した。４-６月期には特許権
の海外譲渡に伴う研究開発投資の減少という特殊要因により押し下げられていたが、７-９月期に
はその影響が剥落したことが前期比での押し上げ要因となった。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を
除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応
した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は引き続き強い。各種調査でも企業の設
備投資計画は高い伸びを維持しており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断
できる。

### Page 4

4/5
先行きについても、輸入資本財価格や建設コストの上昇が設備投資の下押し要因となるもの
の、高水準の企業収益や根強い省力化・デジタル・ＡＩ関連投資需要が下支えとなり、緩やかな
増加基調が続くと予想する。
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れている。
両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減となった。４-６月期には大口要因によって研究開発サービスの
受取が急増していたが、７-９月期にはその反動によってサービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（４-６月
期：＋8.9％）と大幅に減少した。一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（４-６月期：▲0.8％）と持ち
直している。グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体製造装置などの資本財輸出が好
調だったほか、４-６月期に大きく落ち込んだ中東向け輸出に回復の動きがみられたことも財輸出

の押し上げ要因になった。サービス輸出の特殊要因による振れを除けば、海外景気の底堅さとＡ
Ｉ関連需要の拡大を背景に、輸出は緩やかな増加基調を維持していると評価できる。
先行きについても、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さに加え、グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の
拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出を下支えすることから、緩やかな増加基調が続くと
予想する。
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と増加した。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が減少していた
が、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％Pt（前期比年率寄与度▲2.4％Pt）と大幅なマイナスになった。
ただし、輸入増加による成長率の押し下げは、原油などの供給環境が改善したことに伴うもの
であり、需要の弱さを示すものではない。また、輸入増加の裏側では、国家備蓄原油の取り崩し
が一服したことに伴って公的在庫変動が前期比で＋0.5％ポイント、前期比年率では＋2.0％ポイ
ントの大幅プラス寄与となっている。両者は一体として評価する必要があるだろう。

民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.0％Ptと、成長率にはほぼニュートラルだった。製品在庫と原
材料在庫が押し上げに寄与した一方、流通在庫と仕掛品在庫が下押し要因になった。
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と、２四半期連続で減少した。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因となっ
ており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。先行きについても、建設コストの高止まり
や金利上昇の影響から、低調な推移が続く可能性が高い。
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と概ね横ばいとなった。４-６月期に前期比＋1.5％と高い伸びとなっ
た後、増勢が一服している。もっとも、医療・介護関連の支出などは趨勢的に増加しており、政
府消費の弱さは前期の反動による一時的なものと考えられる。
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と減少した。政府の国土強靱化関連工事などが下支えにはなっている
が、建設コストの上昇が実質公共投資の下押し要因になっているとみられる。

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限

Document 096: 2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～.pdf

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年11月 17日

1/3 
2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し 
～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2027年の春闘賃上げ率を、厚生労働省ベースで 5.15％（2026年：5.18％）、連合ベースで
4.98％（同 5.01％）と予測する。2027年春闘でもほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、厚生労
働省ベースでは４年連続で５％台が実現するだろう。連合は 2027 年春闘の要求目安を前年
よりやや引き上げ、経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。ＵＡゼンセ
ンも前年並みの高い要求水準を示すなど、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
○ 構造的な人手不足が続くなか、人材の確保・定着に向けた賃上げの必要性は高い。企業収益
も総じて堅調であり、高水準の利益が賃上げ余力を支えている。2026年の物価上昇率が前年
から鈍化することは賃上げ率の下押し要因となる。一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初に
は物価上昇率が再び高まる可能性があり、実質賃金の安定的な改善を求める労使双方の問題
意識も強い。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続
する環境は維持されている。 
○ ４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、日本銀行にとっても金融政策の正常化に向けて前
向きな材料となる。特に、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃
金を設定するようになっているかどうかが、２％の物価安定目標の定着を見極めるうえで重
要となる。

2027年春闘でも前年並みの高い賃上げ率を予想 
2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 5.15％と予測する（厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結
状況」ベース）。2024年春闘では5.33％、2025年では 5.52％、2026年でも 5.18％と３年連続で
５％台の高い賃上げが実現したが、2027年もほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、４年連続で５％
台となるだろう。 
春闘においては、厚生労働省よりも公表時期が早い、連合による集計値が注目されることが多
い。この連合ベースの数字では、2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 4.98％と予測する（2026年春闘：
5.01％）。５％台に乗るかどうかは微妙なところだが、こちらも歴史的にみれば高い賃上げとなる

### Page 2

2/3 
だろう。

組合側、経営側ともトーンダウンはみられず  
組合側、経営側とも賃上げに前向きな姿勢を維持している。連合は 10月22日に公表した2027
年春闘基本構想において、定昇込みの賃上げ要求を「６％中心、５％以上」、ベースアップ等の賃
上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、前年の賃上げ「５％以上」、ベア「３％以上」よりも要求
姿勢をやや強めた。経営側も賃上げの継続に前向きだ。経団連の経営労働政策委員会報告原案で
は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられていると報じられた。また、筒井経団連会長
は 11月９日の記者会見で、ベア実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードとする方針を堅持する考え
を示した。また、主要産業別労働組合組織では、ＵＡゼンセンが定昇込み６％、ベア等４％とい
う前年と同等の高い要求方針素案を示している。中東情勢悪化によるコスト上昇という逆風があ
るなかでも、労使ともに賃上げ姿勢がトーンダウンしている様子は窺えない。もっとも、連合の
要求目安は強まったものの、ＵＡゼンセンの要求率は前年と同水準であり、現時点では賃上げ率
のさらなる加速を見込むほどの材料が揃っているわけではないことには注意が必要だ。

物価は前年から鈍化も、人手不足と好調な企業収益が押し上げ要因に 
高い賃上げを支える最大の要因は、構造的な人手不足である。生産年齢人口の減少が続くな
か、企業の人手不足感は依然として強く、人材の確保・定着は重要な経営課題となっている。特
に若年層を中心に採用競争が厳しくなっており、既存人材のつなぎ止めのためにも企業は継続的
な賃上げを行う必要がある。ここ数年の春闘では、こうした人手不足を背景に高い賃上げが実現
しており、企業の賃金設定行動にも変化が生じている。2027年春闘でも人手不足を背景とした賃
上げ圧力は強いと考えられる。 
好調な企業収益も、高い賃上げを支える材料になる。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇は企業収
益の下押し要因となっているものの、価格転嫁の進展やＡＩ関連需要の拡大などを背景に、企業
収益は総じて堅調に推移しており、水準も非常に高い。企業規模や業種によって差はあるもの
の、企業収益が全体として高水準にあり、賃上げ余力は十分に確保されていると見てよい。 
一方、物価については、2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化することが賃上げ率の下押し要因
になるとみられる。春闘交渉では前年の暦年の物価上昇率が参照されることが多いが、2026年の
上昇率（ＣＰＩ総合）は前年比＋２％前後と、2025年の前年比＋3.2％から鈍化する見込みであ
り、物価高への対応という観点からの賃上げ圧力は弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、暦年でみれば物価上昇率は鈍化する一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初には再び
高まり、ＣＰＩは前年比＋３％台に達する可能性がある。この場合、実質賃金が再びマイナスに
転じる恐れもある。そもそも、過去数年にわたって実質賃金が減少してきたことで、家計には賃
上げの恩恵を十分に実感できないという不満が残っている。実質賃金の改善の遅れへの問題意識
は組合側、経営側にも共有されており、実質賃金を安定的に増加させることは 2027年春闘でも重
要な課題となるだろう。春闘交渉の時点で物価上昇率の再加速が意識されれば、春闘賃上げ率の

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
上振れ要因となる可能性もあることには注意したい。

２％「定着」判断の重要な材料に  
以上を踏まえ、2027年春闘では5.15％と、ほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げを予想する（厚生労働省
ベース）。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続する環境
は維持されていると考えられる。 
４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、賃金上昇の持続性を重視する日本銀行にとっても前
向きな材料となるだろう。特に重要なのは、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを
前提として賃金を設定するようになっているかどうかである。2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍
化するなかでも高い賃上げが維持されれば、こうした賃金設定が広がりつつあることを示す一つ
の材料となる。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定目標とおおむね整合的な水準に達し
たとの認識を示したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判断していない。2027年春
闘は、その定着を見極めるうえでも重要な判断材料となるだろう。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：   
環境：鴻海採用 TCFD 框架為基礎，全面盤點與評估氣候相關風險與機會，
並導入 ISO 14001 環境管理系統與 ISO 45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，同時
也推動「低碳供應鏈轉型推動計畫」，協助超過 30 家供應商完成節能專案，
累計減少碳排放約 15,000 公噸 CO₂e。鴻海已有超過 150 家供應商完成碳盤
查並簽署科學基礎減碳目標（SBTi），提升能源使用效率與減碳成效。  
社會：鴻海秉持「以人為本」理念，長期支持教育與青年培訓，設立「三創
育成基金會」，提供新創團隊資金與資源支持，自 2015 年以來已經培育超
過  100 組科技創業團隊，促進創新創業生態系發展，為社會培訓科技人才。
治理：董事會有設立「永續發展委員會」，主管由具備三年以上公開發行公
司財務主管經歷之財務長兼任，該委員自 2023 年起每年召開至少四次會議，
全體委員及相關主管亦於任職期間完成 ESG 專業訓練課程，強化永續治理
能力與資訊揭露透明度。 
公司簡介：  
鴻海精密工業股份有限公司（Hon Hai Precision Industry），成立於 1974 年，
由郭台銘創辦，總部位於新北市土城區。公司早期以模具及電子零組件製造
起家，逐步擴展至電子產品組裝、系統整合及設計製造服務，現為全球最大
電子專業製造服務（EMS）廠商。 
集團業務涵蓋消費智能產品、雲端網路產品、電腦終端產品及元件與其他產
品四大領域，旗下包含工業富聯（FII）、鴻騰精密（FIT）等重要子公司，
並透過全球事業單位及子公司分工，提供零組件、模組至整機系統的垂直整
合服務。集團生產及服務據點遍及全球逾 20 個國家及地區，包括中國、台
灣、印度、越南、墨西哥及美國等，全球員工人數於季節性高峰約達 100 萬
人。 
鴻海主要競爭對手包括廣達、緯創、和碩、仁寶及立訊精密等，依產品領域
有所不同。近年積極布局 AI 伺服器、電動車及半導體等新興業務，其中 AI
伺服器受惠全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）資本支出擴張，成為重要成長動能，
並持續鞏固全球電子製造服務龍頭地位。

3 群益投顧 
上升，將使手機出貨面臨壓力，但產品 ASP 提升及 Apple 穩健的品牌效應
仍將為出貨量提供支撐，因此 4Q26 仍將受惠新機推出與傳統旺季帶來的成
長。相較於消費電子的季節性，GB200/300 放量與產品組合變化對全年營運
的影響更大，因此 2026 年的主要成長引擎仍是伺服器業務的擴張；Vera 
Rubin 則為 2027 年的成長建立基礎。綜上所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 3.37
兆元，QoQ+11.7%；毛利率/營益率持續受 Vera Rubin 新平台出貨而稀釋，
下滑 0.10ppts/0.06ppts 來到 5.92%/3.77%，歸屬母公司淨利 899.02 億元，
QoQ+15.97%，稅後 EPS 6.39 元。 
雲端網路成為營運主軸，規模經濟支持本業獲利成長： 3Q26 雲端網路產品
占營收 54%，高於 2Q26 的 51%。消費智能、電腦終端及元件與其他產品分
別占 29%、12%及 5%。雲端網路產品受 AI 伺服器及機櫃出貨帶動，季增與
年增均強勁。消費智能產品亦受新品與傳統旺季帶動，季增優於公司原先預
期。據我們所知，伺服器約占雲端網路營收的 85%-90%，其中約 70%來自
AI 伺服器。隨雲端網路占比提高，AI 機櫃的交付時程、平台組合及物料認
列方式，將更直接影響鴻海的營收與獲利率。鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-
0.1ppts，主要受到整櫃 GPU 伺服器高昂的 BOM 所 稀 釋；營 業 利 益 率 3.83%，
QoQ+0.07ppts，雖然毛利率季減，營益率卻有所改善，反映出營收規模擴大
及營運效率提升的效益。 
Vera Rubin 接力 GB300，2027 年進入新平台主要貢獻期：AI 機櫃的新舊平
台交替是鴻海延續成長的核心動能。鴻海預計 2026 年 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨
較 2025 年倍增以上，且既有 GB 平台需求可能延續至 2027 年。此外，Vera 
Rubin 與 GB 系列同為 NVL72，因此我們預期鴻海將可較平滑的實現世代交
替。管理層預估 4Q26 Vera Rubin出貨量將呈高雙位數增長。展望 2027 年，
伺服器業務上會新增更多 Neocloud 客戶，但主要客戶仍然是大型 CSP。整
體訂單並未隨近期市場的擔憂而調整，對於 AI 投資的展望維持正向，預估
AI 機櫃出貨量將呈高雙位數成長。 
自製零組件與自動化支撐獲利，高 BOM 仍主導毛利率走勢：鴻海透過垂直
整合提高單一專案可承接的製造內容，管理層表示除規模經濟外，零組件自
製率的提高與自動化生產都將為毛利率及營益率提供支撐。根據我們調查，
在晶片以外的零組件自製率約 50%，布局包括連接器、線材、Midplane、交
換器及散熱相關產品，零組件毛利率高於 15%，部分甚至可達 20%，管理層
亦提出 800G 以上交換器全年營收倍增的展望，這些產品有助鴻海由機櫃組
裝向高速互聯及關鍵元件延伸，並可提升附加價值。然而據我們了解目前鴻
海在 GPU 的大型專案中尚未採用 consignment model，記憶體等元件漲價則
主要透過 cost-plus 模式轉嫁給客戶，進入 Vera Rubin 世代後，由於 ASP 將
近倍增，會進一步造成毛利率稀釋，考慮到 GPU Rack 單價極高，我們預估
2027 年毛利率仍將承壓。 
ASIC 拓展產品組合，物料模式與 ASP 差異使營收比重低於出貨量佔比：據
我們了解，鴻海 ASIC 平台約佔 AI 伺服器營收的 20%，較 1H26 略升，且
ASIC 平台成長速度高於 GPU 平台，除既有客戶外亦有新專案，管理層預估
3Q26 ASIC 伺服器出貨量 YoY+150%，高於 GPU 伺服器 70%的出貨量年增。
我們認為 ASIC 專案增加可擴大鴻海在不同運算平台的參與，減少成長來源
集中於單一 GPU 平台的程度。此外，大部分 ASIC 專案將有望採用
consignment model，有助於緩解 AI 伺服器對於整體毛利率的負面效果，然
而 GPU 平台 ASP 顯著高於 ASIC 平台，因此 ASIC 的營收較難完整反映其

4 群益投顧 
專案及製造規模。我們認為目前 ASIC 專案採用 consignment model 尚不足
以顯著改善 GPU Rack 高 BOM 對整體毛利率的稀釋。 
策略投資三菱電機，電動車可望成為未來另一成長引擎：鴻海透過與三菱電
機的策略結盟深化在電動車領域的發展，雖投資金額小於 AI 相關的支出，
但將持續投入研發，目前已做好充足準備量產，將尋機大規模進入 EV 市場，
可望成為未來的另一成長動能。 
預估 2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，稅後 EPS 24.71 元：我們預估隨 AI 機櫃放量
將提高營收與獲利規模，2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，YoY+37.1%，毛利率 5.65%，
YoY-0.4ppts；營業利益 5,181.73 億元，YoY+25.5%，營益率 3.43%，YoY-
0.3ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 3,478.27 億元，YoY+25.4%，稅後 EPS 24.71 元。
Vera Rubin 由初期出貨轉為全年營收貢獻，加上既有 GB300 需求延續及
ASIC 業務擴張，帶動全年營收顯著增長。公司持續布局台灣、美國、墨西
哥及越南產能，亦有助因應客戶在不同地區的交付需求。 
投資建議：受惠 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，2026 AI 機櫃出貨可望倍數增長。
此外我們預期憑藉 iPhone 的品牌效應，4Q26 智慧型消費電子業務可望維持
過往的傳統出貨旺季。展望 2027 年，Vera Rubin 將成為主要成長引擎，主
要客戶對於 AI 伺服器的需求依舊強勁。關鍵製程自動化及零組件自製率的
提升亦有助提升營運效率，為毛利率及營益率提供部分支撐。考量上述原
因，我們給予鴻海 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

M
Update
Lotes Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
Post-AP Summit Taiwan
Corporate Day Feedback
Key takeaways from Lotes:
• Revenue mix ~85% China / ~15% Vietnam. Vietnam is a full function factory,
with Phase I (3 buildings) all under production now, and with more land to
expand for Phase II. Management sees a path for Vietnam to rise to ~20% of
revenue contribution.
• Server is the main growth contributor next year with ~60% y/y sales growth,
driven by a combination of volume (10-15%), ASP (20%+), and new products

(QD, SOCAMM, NPO, etc).
• Management expects 2027 revenue growth to accelerate vs 2026 (1-3Q26
revenue is up 17.7% y/y), driven mainly by new CPU socket adoption increase,
and new products ramp.
• Lotes expects QD revenue to rise to ~10% of revenue contribution in CY27,
from 3-5% currently. Current supply share is still small.
• Lotes is one of the first suppliers for SOCAMM connectors, and management
expects revenue to continue to grow in CY27, from 2-3% of revenue.

• As per management, NPO socket should start to contribute to revenue in
2H27, with more material growth expected in CY28, with adoption across
multiple models.
• Management noted PC revenue declined to 22% of total revenue as of 3Q26
from 36% in CY25, suggesting that further declines in the PC market in CY27
could have a limited impact to Lotes.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lotes Co. Ltd. (3533.TW)
Primary - Residual Income
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.6% (beta of
1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate of
12.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected growth in the server business, such as from cloud and enter-
prise in China

n Stronger-than-expected DT demand
n Sooner-than-expected inventory digestion of NB
n Faster ramp-up of its EV business
Risks to Downside
n Further deceleration of server demand from US hyperscalers
n Weaker-than-expected DT demand
n Slower-than-expected inventory digestion of NB
n More severe pricing erosion driven by competition

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

BUY (M)

목표주가 129,000원 (D)  
 직전 목표주가 190,000원  
 현재주가 (10/29) 108,300원  
 상승여력 19%

시가총액 422,817억원  
 총발행주식수 390,412,998주  
 60일 평균 거래대금 947억원  
 60일 평균 거래량 776,434주  
 52주 고/저 206,000원 / 105,100원  
 외인지분율 39.05%  
 배당수익률 0.00%  
 주요주주 현대자동차 외 5 인

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (6.6) (10.0) (6.5)  
 상대 8.0  (14.2) (35.7)  
 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.11.02  
잠시 쉬어갑니다

3Q26 Review 
기아는 3Q26P 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.4% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회했다. 영업이익 증
감 요인은 환율 효과(+5,290억원), 물량 효과(+4,420억원), 믹스 효과(-4,650억원), 
인센티브/가격 효과(-7,270억원), 재료비 영향(-3,680억원), 기타(+4,130억원)이다. 
판매 성장에도 높아진 수익성 부담 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.9% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.1만대
(+8.8%)를 기록했다. 미국 HEV와 서유럽 EV 판매가 각각 118%, 163% 증가하며 
글로벌 점유율은 4.0%까지 확대됐다. 다만, 판매 성장에도 원화 강세와 원자재 가격 
상승, EV 비중 확대에 따른 믹스 부담으로 수익성은 전분기 대비 하락했다. 유럽 내 
EV 판매가 늘어나면서 믹스가 악화되었으며, 전쟁으로 인한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 
재료비 부담은 전년 대비 약 3,700억 증가했다.  
연간 판매목표 335만대는 유지했으나, 4분기 수익성의 급격한 반등은 어려울 전망이
다. 향후 미국 텔루라이드·스포티지 HEV 공급 확대와 유럽 EV 판매 증가에 따른 고정
비 흡수가 수익성 회복의 핵심이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 155,000원으로 하향 
기아에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 실적 추정치 하향을 반영해 목표주가를 
155,000원으로 하향한다. 재료비 부담, 환율 효과 축소, 예상보다 큰 믹스 악화로 단
기 이익 눈높이 조정은 불가피하다. 다만 산업수요 둔화에도 주요 시장에서 점유율을 
확대되고 있다는 점은 긍정적이다. 현재 배당 수익률은 7% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높
은 배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

Document 289: AI收入破亿后，OceanBase寻找数据库业务新入口

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI收入破亿后，OceanBase寻找数据库业务新入口.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 24 ⽇，蚂蚁集团旗下数据库公司 OceanBase 在北京举办 AI 数据⼤会。公司披露，
2026 年 AI 业务收⼊⾸次突破 1 亿元。 OceanBase CEO 杨冰表⽰，按全量数据⼝径，⽼客
⼾约占 AI 收⼊的七成；按客⼾数量计，新客⼾占⽐约 40% 。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 24 ⽇，蚂蚁集团旗下数据库公司 OceanBase 在北京举办 AI 数据⼤会。公司披露， 2026 年 AI
业务收⼊⾸次突破 1 亿元。
OceanBase CEO 杨冰在会后群访中对华尔街⻅闻等媒体表⽰， AI 相关营收中⽼客⼾贡献更⼤。
按全量数据⼝径，⽼客⼾约占 AI 收⼊的七成；按客⼾数量计，新客⼾占⽐约 40% ，但新客⼾单体
量较⼩、上量⽐⽼客⼾慢。
从这⼀结构来看， OceanBase 当前 AI 业务的增⻓中，存量客⼾是重要的组成部分。对于已经使
⽤ OceanBase 数据库的⾦融、政企客⼾⽽⾔， AI 应⽤落地可能带来向量检索、知识库、 Agent 等
新增需求，进⽽形成新的收⼊来源。

相⽐之下，新客⼾数量虽已占相当⽐例，收⼊端的兑现还需要时间。
这⼀亿元收⼊的构成，杨冰在群访中拆成了三块。
⼀是软件产品，包括向量、 RAG 知识库、 Agent 底座、图库及多 workload 融合⽅案；⼆是服务，
包括 FDE 团队交付与轻咨询；三是智算，公共云上调⽤模型，并为具⾝智能客⼾提供部分算
⼒。杨冰称这⼀结构还不够成熟，未来更趋向以软件和算⼒为主。
从⽬前的收⼊构成看， OceanBase 的 AI 业务还未形成以标准化软件产品为核⼼的成熟商业模
式，服务交付和算⼒调⽤也在贡献收⼊；结构向软件和算⼒倾斜，可能对应产品化收⼊占⽐的
提升。
参照⾏业体量，信通院统计 2025 年中国数据库市场规模约 678 亿元。 OceanBase 整体营收未披
露， 1 亿元 AI 收⼊对公司的意义更多是起点，⽽⾮规模贡献。
关于 AI 产品的续约和复购情况，⽬前 OceanBase 未披露具体数据，能够参考的是数据库的续约
和复购。
OceanBase 副总裁、政企事业部总经理丁荣介绍，在政企客⼾的数据库选型流程中，部分客⼾

使⽤全量数据做 POC 验证，并考察数据库⼚商的经营存续能⼒。
按照丁荣的说法，政企客⼾续约复购的关键因素是拥有成本，客⼾续约时计算的是包含运维与
扩容在内的整体成本。丁荣提到， OceanBase 在整体设计⽅案中能帮助客⼾把计算成本节省三
分之⼀、存储成本节省⼆分之⼀。
谈及竞争格局，杨冰预计，政企业务营收 2027 年有望超越⾦融业务。
OceanBase CTO 杨传辉补充， “ 有很多客⼾因为先选 OceanBase AI 类产品，再选 OceanBase 数
据库本⾝，是反过来的 ” ，过去是数据库带 AI 产品，现在是 AI 产品带数据库，⼊⼝变了。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second
consecutive shock in October, pushing annual
employment growth well below working-age

population growth."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
